國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟成長放緩但仍具韌性，通膨居高，消費信心轉弱，Fed 維持鷹派立場 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率 1.5%，低於第 1 季 2.1%，顯示
經濟成長略趨緩。消費支出貢獻 2.31 個百分點為主要動能，惟淨出口、
庫存調整及政府支出轉弱形成拖累；內需仍具韌性，但通膨壓力尚未明
顯緩解。 
⚫ 8 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 55.2 降至 51.7，月減 6.3%、年減
11.2%，現況與預期指數同步下滑。調查指出，高物價、汽油價格預期
及政策不確定性壓抑信心；一年通膨預期由 4.2%降至 4.0%，長期預期
持平 3.3%。 
⚫ 7 月美國新屋銷售年化率 60.7 萬套，較上月及去年同期分別減少 10.5%
及 6.3%；房價中位數續跌，供給月數升至 9.6 個月，反映銷售動能降
溫，庫存去化速度放緩，整體市場供給仍維持相對充裕。 
⚫ 7 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.2%，由前月-0.1%回升，年增率持平於
3.7%；核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、年增 3.3%，顯示通膨壓力仍具黏著性。另
一方面，個人所得及可支配所得分別月增 0.4%及 0.5%，惟實質消費支
出由前月成長 0.4%放緩至持平，儲蓄率則升至 3.0%，顯示內需動能略
為降溫。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美加貿易爭端持續升溫 ， 美國自8 月 22 日起對加拿大部分商品加徵 50%
關稅；加拿大則公布涵蓋約700 項、總值276 億美元美國商品的報復性
關稅措施，將於 9 月 8 日生效。此外，川普表示擬於 2027 年將加拿大
汽車及鋼鐵關稅提高至 50%。 整體而言 ， 美加已進入實質貿易報復階段 ，
恐增加北美供應鏈與經貿不確定性。 
⚫ 川普於 8 月 26 日簽署第 14420 號行政命令，針對外國製大規模電力系
統設備可能帶來的資安漏洞 、 遠端操控及供應鏈中斷等國安風險宣布國
家緊急狀態，並授權能源部對高風險設備的採購、進口、轉讓及安裝進
行審查、限制或附加條件管理，以強化美國電網安全與供應鏈韌性；能
源部須於 120 天內完成相關執行規則制定。 
⚫ Fed 主席華許於 Jackson Hole 首場演說釋出偏鷹訊號，指美國通膨仍高
於 Fed 2%目標， 若未明確回落， 仍保留升息空間； 同時主張淡化前瞻指

⚫ 美國第 2 季經濟成長放緩至 1.5%， 消費與固定投資仍為主要支撐動能 ，

貨幣政策走向面臨較高不確定性。

東京通膨升溫，潛在物價壓力持續增強

⚫ 歐盟執委會主席馮德萊恩警告，若 10 月前歐中經貿談判無法取得實質
成果，歐盟不排除運用被稱為「貿易火箭」的反脅迫機制，進行進出口

⚫ 美國財政部長貝森特於 8 月 30 日公開呼籲 G20 國家重新檢視對中貿易
條件，並藉由提高貿易壁壘促使中國調整以出口導向為主的經濟模式。 
短評 ⚫ 展望未來，在歐美同步關注中國龐大貿易順差的情況下，中國貿易環境
面臨的阻力恐持續增加，亦不利於工業部門發展。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.8.31 
一、 美國—經濟成長放緩但仍具韌性，通膨居高，消費信心轉弱，Fed 維持鷹
派立場 
美國商務部經濟分析局(BEA)公布 2026 年第 2 季 GDP 第二次估值，美國第
2 季實質 GDP 季增年率為 1.5%，與初估值相同，但低於第 1 季的 2.1%，顯示經
濟維持溫和擴張但成長動能略為放緩。個人消費支出為主要成長動能，貢獻 2.31
個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.42 及 0.89 個百分點，反映民間需求
仍具韌性；私人投資則貢獻0.48 個百分點，其中設備投資及智慧財產產品投資表
現穩健，但受庫存調整拖累部分成長。另一方面，淨出口拖累經濟成長1.14 個百
分點，主因進口大幅增加抵銷出口成長效益；政府消費支出及投資亦負貢獻 0.16
個百分點。值得注意的是，反映內需基本面的實質私人國內最終銷售成長4.2%，
顯示民間需求仍具支撐力。整體而言，第 2 季經濟成長主要由消費及固定投資帶
動 ， 但受進口增加 、 庫存調整及政府支出下滑影響 ， 成長速度較前季放緩 ； 此外 ，
PCE 物價指數及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.3%及 3.6%，顯示通膨壓力仍偏
高，後續仍須關注消費、投資及通膨發展對經濟前景之影響。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 8 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 51.7，低於 7 月
的 55.2，月減 6.3%、年減 11.2%，顯示民眾信心在 7 月回升後再度轉弱；其中，
當前經濟狀況指數由 54.8 降至 51.9，月減 5.3%、年減 15.9%，消費者預期指數亦
由 55.4 降至 51.5，月減 7.0%、年減 7.9%，反映民眾對現況與未來景氣看法同步
惡化。調查指出，8 月信心下滑涵蓋各政治族群，其中共和黨人降幅尤為明顯，
且高齡、低中所得及未持股等較難承受生活成本上升的消費者信心降幅較大；在
包含伊朗衝突在內的政策不確定性下，消費者預期汽油價格短期及長期仍將上升，
並更加擔憂經濟其他領域前景轉弱， 未來一年與五年商業環境預期分別下滑10%
及 13%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨預期由 7 月 4.2%降至 4.0%，但仍高於伊
朗衝突前 2 月的 3.4%及所有 2024 年讀數；長期通膨預期則連續第三個月持平於
3.3%，仍略高於 2024 年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，8 月美國消費信心再度
降溫，顯示高物價、汽油價格預期與政策不確定性仍壓抑民眾信心，且若貿易緊
張再升級，可能進一步加劇消費者悲觀情緒。

5

2026 年 7 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 60.7 萬套，較上月減少
10.5%，亦較去年同期衰退 6.3%；地區銷售表現分歧，其中東北部及西部分別較
上月增加 30.3%及 6.2%，中西部及南部則分別減少 42.7%及 13.0%。價格方面，
7 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.38 萬美元，月減 2.3%、年減 0.9%。供給方面，7 月底
經季節性調整後待售新屋數量為 48.8 萬套，較 6 月底增加 1.9%，但較去年同期
減少 1.6%；按目前銷售速度估算，供給月數由 6 月的 8.5 個月升至 9.6 個月，亦
高於去年同期的 9.2 個月 。 整體而言 ，7 月美國新屋銷售較上月及去年同期下滑 ，
中位房價續降，庫存及供給月數則有所增加，顯示在銷售放緩下，市場庫存去化
速度趨緩，整體供給仍維持相對充裕水準。 
2026 年 7 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由 6 月的-0.1%回升至
0.2%，年增率則持平於 3.7%；扣除食品與能源後之核心 PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至
0.2%，年增率亦維持在 3.3%，顯示整體通膨壓力雖未進一步升高，但月度物價漲
勢較前月略有回溫。另一方面，個人所得及可支配所得分別月增 0.4%及 0.5%，
均高於上月表現 ， 實質可支配所得亦成長0.4%； 惟實質個人消費支出由上月月增
0.4%放緩至持平 ， 顯示民間消費動能略有降溫 。 在所得增幅高於消費增幅帶動下 ，
個人儲蓄率由 2.6%升至 3.0%。整體而言，7 月美國通膨表現大致持穩、所得持續
增長且儲蓄意願提升，但消費支出趨於平緩，顯示內需動能較前月略為放緩，後
續仍須持續關注通膨走勢、消費表現及其對貨幣政策之影響。 
美加貿易爭端於 8 月持續升溫 ， 在美加貿易談判於8 月 21 日破局後 ， 美國依
《貿易法》第 338 條對加拿大汽車、酒類、乳製品、家具等產品加徵 50%關稅措
施已於 8 月 22 日正式生效，加拿大總理卡尼(Mark Carney) 則表示將採取對等反
制措施。8 月 25 日，加拿大財政部公布完整報復性關稅清單，涵蓋約700 項美國
商品、總值約 276 億美元，依照美國關稅稅率對應課徵 15%、25%及 50%不等的
反制關稅，其中鋼鋁產品稅率提高至 50%，部分家電、乳製品及鋼鋁衍生產品課
徵 25%，電機設備及工具則課徵 15%，預計於 9 月 8 日正式生效，並同步推出數
十億美元規模的勞工及企業支援方案。此外，川普總統於 8 月 24 日透過社群媒
體表示，擬自 2027 年 1 月 1 日起將加拿大汽車、卡車、汽車零組件及鋼鐵產品
關稅由現行 25%提高至 50%，惟截至目前尚未發布正式行政命令。整體而言，美
加關稅衝突已由雙邊談判擴大至實質貿易報復階段，未來若相關措施全面落實，
恐進一步增加北美供應鏈成本與區域經貿不確定性。

### Page 7

用相關內容。

6

美國總統川普於 8 月 26 日簽署第 14420 號行政命令 ， 依據 《國際緊急經濟權
力法》 （IEEPA） 及 《國家緊急狀態法》 （NEA） ， 就外國供應大型電力系統 （Bulk-
Power System ）設備可能衍生的資安漏洞、遠端存取、惡意操作、蓄意破壞及供
應鏈中斷風險宣布國家緊急狀態。該命令授權美國能源部對涉及國家安全風險的
外國製電力設備之採購 、 進口 、轉讓及安裝實施限制或審查 ，適用範圍涵蓋69 千
伏以上輸電系統，以及維持電力系統可靠運作所需的發電與控制設施，包括變壓
器、逆變器、儲能系統、發電與工業控制設備，以及相關軟體、韌體與遠端維護
服務，但不包括地方配電設施。行政命令要求能源部於 120 天內制定執行規則，
並於 180 天內提出聯邦採購規範修正建議，以降低外國供應鏈風險、強化美國電
網安全與關鍵基礎設施韌性，並使聯邦能源基礎設施採購優先考量國家安全與美
國製造設備。 
Fed 新任主席華許(Kevin Warsh)於 8 月 28 日在 Jackson Hole 全球央行年會發
表上任後首場主題演說，釋出偏鷹派訊號。他表示，美國通膨雖已自高點回落，
但 7 月 PCE 年增率仍達 3.7%，明顯高於聯準會(Fed)2%目標，近期通膨數據改善
仍不足以證明潛在通膨趨勢已實質好轉；若決策官員無法確認通膨正以明確且足
夠的速度回落至目標水準，Fed「仍有工作要做」 ，市場因而解讀為未來仍保留進
一步升息空間。華許同時主張淡化前瞻指引(Forward Guidance)，降低市場對利率
預測與點陣圖的依賴，打造「更安靜的聯準會」，並強調貨幣政策應保有彈性，
依據即時經濟數據、趨勢及金融情勢作出判斷。在人工智慧 (AI)方面，華許認為
AI 可能成為新的生產要素，有助推升長期生產力與潛在成長率，但其對通膨、就
業市場及貨幣政策的影響仍有待觀察。 
二、歐元區—經濟增溫，歐洲央行 9 月升息預期增加 
 歐元區 7 月廣義貨幣供給(M3)年增率增加 0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，長天期存款
(M3-M2)年增率則降 0.2 個百分點至 4.2%；另外，家庭貸款年增率增加 0.1 個百
分點至 3%、非金融企業貸款餘額增加 0.4 個百分點至 4.4%。整體而言，家庭與
企業融資需求持續擴張，加上經濟表現優於預期，帶動貨幣供給穩步增長，亦為
歐洲央行 9 月進一步升息提供政策空間。 
歐元區 8 月經濟景氣指數增加 1.3 點至 98.4，反映消費者與企業信心同步改
善。其中，零售業及服務業信心分別上升 0.7 點與 1.1 點，顯示內需活動與服務

需求有所回溫 ； 此外 ， 就業預期指數增加1.5 點至 98.9， 企業聘僱計畫轉趨積極 ，
勞動市場亦呈現改善跡象。在消費、服務活動及就業情勢逐步好轉的帶動下，歐
元區經濟動能持續增溫。 
由於中國廉價商品大量湧入歐盟，對歐洲製造業形成壓力，歐盟對中國的貿
易逆差已接近每日 10 億歐元，因此歐盟委員會主席馮德萊恩在 8 月 27 日表示，
若歐中貿易談判未能有效改善雙方貿易失衡問題，歐盟不排除進一步運用反脅迫
機制及貿易防衛工具。此舉反映歐盟對中國產能過剩、補貼政策及關鍵供應鏈依
賴的疑慮持續升高，未來雙方貿易摩擦恐有進一步升溫風險。 
三、日本—東京通膨升溫，潛在物價壓力持續增強 
8 月東京核心通膨增加 0.1 個百分點至 1.8%，雖然政府透過電力、天然氣補
貼及免費托兒措施抑制部分物價漲幅，但扣除相關政策因素後，核心通膨（扣除
生鮮食品）及核心核心通膨（扣除生鮮食品及能源）均已高於日本央行 2%的政
策目標。從細項來看，家具、家用品等商品價格持續攀升；食品價格雖因米價回
落而略有緩和，但外食價格年增 5.0%，反映食品相關價格壓力尚未明顯消退；醫
療服務及租金漲幅均超過 2%，顯示通膨壓力已由商品擴散至服務領域。整體而
言 ， 潛在通膨動能持續增強 ， 若未來政府補貼措施逐步退場 ， 通膨仍有上行風險 ，
將提高央行緊縮貨幣政策的壓力。 
四、中國—歐美對中國貿易圍堵壓力持續增加 
1-7 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.5%、17.6%，其中營業收入較
上期(1-6 月)持平，利潤增速則降 1.1 個百分點。受惠於金屬、塑化原物料上漲，
加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因素，營收成長貢獻度較高
者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 19.6%、91.8%)、計算機、通信和其他電子
設備製造業(營收 、 利潤年增19.4%、105%)、 化學原料及製品(營收 、 利潤年增9.3%、
56.6%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年增率雖分別為 10%、2.7%，但
獲利分別年減 7.6%、20.4%，顯示產業陷入價格競爭壓力，使利潤受到擠壓。非
金屬礦物製品業營收、獲利下滑13.7、48.2%，反映產能過剩情形嚴重；黑色金屬
加工業營收雖下滑 6.4%，惟獲利止跌回升 2%，反映鋼價暫時回升。整體而言，
部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較
佳，惟其他多數產業營收獲利表現較為平淡，產業分化趨勢明顯。

展望未來， 歐盟近期已針對從中國進口的電池 、 輪胎 、 紡織原料等展開調查 ，

持續增加，亦不利於工業部門發展。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓央行政策分歧，7 月貿易續呈逆差 
(1) 新加坡：7 月消費者物價指數年增率升至 2.2%，為 2024 年 8 月以來的最高，
其中食品 （2.2%） 、住房和水電 （1.3%） 、 交通 （7.9%） 以及醫療保健 （3.3%）
的價格漲幅加快 ； 剔除住宿和私人交通成本的核心通膨率從1.6%上升至 2%，
整體而言 ， 通膨上揚主要受公用事業價格上漲的推動 ， 商品和服務價格仍保持
穩定。 
(2) 泰國：泰國央行 8 月會議維持政策利率於 1%不變，保持寬鬆的貨幣政策以支
持經濟復甦 ， 並強調金融機構應增加對弱勢群體和潛在中小企業的支持性 。 央
行表示，經濟成長依然緩慢且不均衡，由於生活成本上升，家庭保持謹慎，私
人消費疲軟 ， 但受強於預期的商品出口和與科技及人工智慧週期相關的私人投
資所支撐，預計 2026 年及 2027 年經濟成長為 2.3%、1.8%；通膨方面，受聖
嬰現象及能源價格上漲傳導效應，預估至 2027 年第一季前將上揚，隨後將因
疲弱的國內需求及較低的潛在經濟成長而下滑。 
7 月出口額年增 21.6%(348 億美元)， 主要由工業產品出口成長24.2%所帶動 ，
其中電腦及相關設備（63.2%） 、電話及零件 （160.3%）及電路板 （35.2%）增
幅最為顯著 ， 對美國出口年增45.3%、 對中國和日本分別成長15.2%、21.3%。
7 月進口額年增 36.7%(384 億美元)，其中燃料、原材料和中間產品、資本貨物
進口成長達均逾 40%以上。進出口相抵，7 月貿易逆差達 36.1 億美元，為連續
第九個月出現逆差。 
(3) 菲律賓:菲律賓央行 8 月會議升息 1 碼至 5.0%，旨在抑制持續的通膨壓力。央
行表示，儘管通膨有所緩和，但油價波動、聖嬰現象對農業的影響及潛在的工
資調整仍構成上行風險 ， 預計2026 年和 2027 年平均整體通膨率仍將突破 4.0%
的容忍上限，2028 年才會回落至 3.0%的目標水準。不過，央行對經濟成長前

六、台灣—景氣對策信號連八月呈紅燈 
7 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與上月持平，其中製造
業營業氣候測驗點受惠於人工智慧需求強勁，由綠燈轉呈黃紅燈；貨幣總計數
M1B 受外資淨匯出等影響，由黃紅燈轉呈綠燈，其他七項燈號維持不變。另領先
與同時指標皆呈上升，顯示國內經濟動能維持穩健。

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: Tokenization expands into market infrastructure
Market share of tokenized assets, Sep 14 2026
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Sep 14, 2026. “Distributed” denotes tokens
transferable outside the issuing platform and between wallets; it does not measure trading activity. Note:
Credit combines asset-backed credit, corporate credit, diversified credit and specialty finance. Other

assets comprise non-US government debt, private equity, venture capital and real estate.
– The global tokenized assets market, excluding stablecoins, grew from
~$10bn in Jan 2025 to ~$39bn in Sep 2026. US Treasury debt remains the
largest segment outside stablecoins, with money market funds leading
near-term adoption. Despite the US Digital Asset Market Clarity Act failing
to advance through to the Senate yesterday, we remain positive about the
momentum to tokenize both assets and capital markets in the coming

years.
– Institutions are increasingly applying tokenization to settlement, funding
and collateral management. Notable examples include DTCC’s tokenization
service, DL T-based intraday repo and collateral initiatives, and the
Eurosystem’s Pontes solution, which connects DL T platforms to TARGET
Services and is expected to launch later this month.
– A broad tokenized capital market requires progress across three areas:
assets, settlement money and market infrastructure. The tokenized RWA

market remains small relative to traditional markets, and token issuance
does not automatically create secondary-market liquidity.
– In this chartbook, we broaden our focus from tokenized assets, such as
bonds and stocks, to the infrastructure through which they are issued; and
review (i) market size; (ii) adoption trends; (iii) the regulatory drivers of
tokenization; and (iv) barriers that will determine the pace of growth.

– Tokenization records assets or claims as digital tokens on a
distributed ledger, using public or permissioned networks.
– Potential benefits include faster transfers, automated servicing and
smaller investment units.
– Shared records can reduce reconciliation, but trading hours,
redemption terms and investor access remain product-specific.
Two issuance models:
– On-chain (or native): The security or fund share is issued directly on a

distributed ledger.
– Off-chain (or tokenized representation): The underlying asset
remains outside the blockchain, but ownership claims and economic
rights are represented digitally on-chain.

STABLECOIN SPOTLIGHT: The settlement side of tokenization
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s FX Special Report: Tokenised money and the future of FX -
Deutsche Bank Research
– Stablecoins account for ~89% of the combined value of stablecoin and
distributed tokenized assets. We assess them separately because their
principal role is payment and settlement.
– They support near-instant, round-the-clock transfers for remittances,

business payments and treasury operations. Cross-border savings depend on
transaction fees, FX costs, compliance and conversion into bank money.
– Chainalysis projects adjusted annual transfer volume could rise from $28tn in
2025 to $719tn by 2035; higher scenario approaching $1.5 quadrillion.
Regulation and competition
– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
The GENIUS Act takes effect on the earlier of 18 January, 2027, or 120 days

after final implementing regulations are issued.
– Stablecoins could increase competition for bank deposits and payments.
Exchange rewards are distinct from issuer-paid interest, which GENIUS
prohibits for permitted payment stablecoins.
Risks
– Trust depends on reserve quality, legal redemption rights and operational
resilience. Stablecoins remain dependent on their issuers and supporting
institutions.
Tokenized deposits and central bank money provide alternative settlement options. Connecting the cash and securities legs of a transaction can improve settlement

efficiency and reduce fragmentation.
For more on tokenized money and settlement, particularly for FX, read here.

interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?

A more integrated post-trade process
5

### Page 6

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
– Early efforts focused on tokenized claims on traditional securities ( i.e. BlackRock’s
BUIDL, Franklin Templeton FOBXX), while the underlying assets remained off -chain.
– Financial institutions are now extending tokenization to the infrastructure through
which assets are distributed, traded, settled and financed.
DLT adoption: small, but intraday repo offers a practical use case

– Global DLT fixed-income issuance reached €4.8bn in 2025, up 48% from 2024.
– DLT intraday repo (i.e. JPM Kinexys and Broadridge’s DLR) can accelerate collateral
transfers and settlement, helping institutions manage short-term funding needs.
– A Finadium study estimated that using intraday DLR for 15% of activity could reduce
intraday liquidity buffer needs by 8–17%.
Tokenized securities on blockchain rails
– DTCC to launch its tokenization service in Oct 2026, covering Russell 1000 constituents,

ETFs tracking major indices, and US Treasury bills, notes and bonds.
– Initial production trades took place in July with >30 participating firms. Tokens are
recorded on DTCC's Hyperledger Besu network or on Canton, settling on either. The
SEC granted a three-year authorization.
Official-sector initiatives and interoperability
– Project Agorá, led by the BIS and IIF with central banks and financial institutions,
demonstrated a prototype for cross-border settlement using tokenized deposits and

central bank reserves. Real-value testing is the next phase.
– Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a joint interoperability framework with
BCG in March 2026 to address fragmentation across digital asset markets.
– Eurosystem’s Pontes solution connects DLT platforms to TARGET Services for
settlement in central bank money.

SHORT-TERM ADOPTION: Tokenized Treasuries and money market funds lead
Tokenized US Treasury market value reaches $15bn– Tokenized cash-management funds, including BUIDL and USYC,
combine short-term yields with more flexible transfers and collateral

use.
– Potential benefits include faster settlement and lower transaction costs,
making Treasury exposure more accessible across eligible digital
platforms.
– Direct Treasury bond tokenization accounted for 27% of overall US
Treasury tokenization in 2025, indicating adoption beyond fund
structures.
– These assets can also serve as collateral in repo and margin
arrangement, supporting demand beyond investment returns.
Secondary-market constraints
– Tokenized bonds are often held to maturity. Limited investor

participation and weak interoperability between platforms constrain
secondary-market liquidity.
– Some exchanges are attempting to improve liquidity by connecting
digital and conventional markets through dual listings, including SIX
digital exchange and Swiss Stock exchange).
Risks
– Redemption terms can differ from the settlement hours and liquidity of
underlying assets, creating potential liquidity mismatches.
– Blockchain transparency can improve visibility but may also accelerate

withdrawals during stress.
– Using tokenized fund shares across multiple products and as collateral
can create additional channels of contagion.

MEDIUM-TERM ADOPTION: Tokenized equities remain small; DTCC’s launch could
broaden adoption
Market value of tokenized equities
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data up to Sep 10, 2026.
– Tokenized equities remain small (<1%) relative to traditional stock markets.

– 68% of equity tokenization in 2025 consisted of direct tokenization of
individual stocks or baskets of stocks, vs. tokenized equity funds.
– Suggests that blockchain adoption is increasingly focused on the securities
themselves (i.e. DTCC method) rather than through third-party platforms (i.e.
Ondo and xStocks).
Developments
– DTCC’s tokenization service, scheduled to launch in Oct, will allow eligible
existing securities to move across blockchain infrastructure within its

regulated framework.
– These tokenized securities will retain the entitlements, investor protections
and ownership rights associated with their traditional form.
– NYSE has announced plans for a tokenized securities platform designed to
support 24/7 trading of US-listed equities and ETFs, with near-instant
settlement and stablecoin funding.
Benefits: Reduce barriers for foreign investors; automated post-trade processes
and longer trading hours; reduce information asymmetry (e.g., embedding listing

Risks & limitations
– Tokenized equities require clear legal ownership, enforceable investor rights
and alignment with existing securities rules.
– Under some third-party wrapper structures, the token provides exposure to a
share without making its holder a shareholder in the underlying company.
– Investors may therefore face issuer or custodian risk and may not receive the
same voting, dividend or other rights as direct shareholders.

LONG-TERM ADOPTION: Private credit expands through record-keeping; real
estate is growing fast but from a negligeable base
Real estate tokenization process
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Updated September 10. Excludes platforms that issues tokens using blockchain as a
recordkeeping layer ( i.e. Represented Layer)
Private credit
– Private credit stands ~$8bn in total, including asset-backed credit ($2.6bn),

specialty finance ($2.5bn), corporate credit ($1.9bn), and diversified credit
($798mn).
– Activity includes lending linked to digital assets and selected corporate credit
products.
– A larger use case is blockchain-based record-keeping: represented credit stands
at ~$35bn, over 4x the distributed market.
– Companies like Figure Technologies and Tradable use blockchains to support
loan administration, ownership records, payments tracking, and servicing.

Challenges
– Tokenization remains concentrated among a small number of issuers and lacks a
deep, and liquid secondary market.
– Legal fragmentation, credit quality and servicing arrangements remain
important risks. Blockchain records do not remove the underlying borrower’s
default risk.
Tokenized credit market stands at ~$8bnReal estate
– The market expanded from ~$35mn in 2025 to $226mn in Sep 2026, although it
remains small.
Benefits
– Fractional ownership can lower minimum investment sizes (typically for $100-

$1,000 per unit) and widen access, particularly where traditional capital markets
and mortgage markets are limited.
Challenges
– A BIS study found that liquidity improvements can depend on platforms offering
to buy tokens back. These arrangements can help investors exit but expose
platforms to liquidity and solvency risks.

applications; focus is on capital market infrastructure modernization.

UK Cryptoassets and Stablecoins Regime finalised in 2026. FCA and BoE launched the Digital
Securities Sandbox for firms to test DLT-based blockchains; HSBC first bank to go live. The
FCA has also identified fund tokenization as a strategic priority through 2025-2030. FCA/BoE

launched joint vision for wholesale tokenised markets in 2026 – work ongoing. DIGIT / GBTD
development ongoing.
GENIUS Act to begin in Jan 2027 with
final rule out by Nov 26; CLARITY Act
stalling
GENIUS establishes a federal framework for stablecoins. Key controversy is whether yield or
reward mechanisms should be permitted in stablecoins and other digital assets; oversight is
fragmented across the SEC, CFTC, banking regulators and state authorities. CLARITY Act,

market structure bill, addressing yield proposal and related items failed its procedural vote in
Sep. Attention will refocus on US federal regulators for digital asset rules in absence of market
structure.
China opposes permissionless crypto, not
tokenization itself
Strict rules on cryptocurrencies remain; China’s focus remains on e-CNY and state-approved
blockchain networks. Recent reforms suggest the e-CNY is moving away from CBDC towards
a tokenised deposits model, reflecting limited retail CBDC uptake alongside existing mobile

payment platforms solutions.
Project Ensemble Sandbox (Aug 2024);
new regulatory roadmap for digital assets
(Feb 2025); progress ongoing.
HKMA has supported tokenized green bonds; tokenized deposits; tokenized money-market
and investment products. Project Ensemble has progressed to real-value transaction testing
using tokenised deposits. HK to announce a new digital bond platform in H22026 to support
issuance and settlement of tokenized bonds.
Project Guardian (c. 2022)

Institutional tokenization sandbox; money market funds and bonds are tokenized and settled
on-chain. Focus is increasingly on commercialisation and scaling successful pilot projects
across funds, bonds, deposits and FX.

regulated financial instruments.
Tokenized securities classified as “digital securities” under ADGM. Actively supports

BARRIERS TO ADOPTION: Weak secondary market liquidity and lack of regulations
continue to challenge adoption
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research.

10
Main barriers to adoption
– Secondary-market liquidity remains low. Issuance
growth has not consistently translated into active
trading or deep markets.
– Regulatory uncertainty: Fragmented or incomplete
frameworks slow institutional adoption and complicate
integration with traditional financial infrastructure.
– Legal uncertainty persists over whether token
ownership conveys ownership of the underlying
asset or a claim on it.
– In segments like money market funds, rapid

cross-border growth can increase regulatory
arbitrage, capital-flow volatility and AML/CFT
compliance challenges. Policymakers must
adapt frameworks as the technology evolves.
– Fragmented infrastructure: Public blockchains,
permissioned institutional ledgers and hybrid platforms
often lack interoperability, fragmenting markets.
Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a joint
framework to address this in March 2026.
– Limited integration with existing custody, settlement,

and record-keeping arrangements prevents institutions
from scaling tokenized products.
– Proprietary infrastructure and centralized
controls also raise questions about ownership
and governance.
10

### Page 11

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
OUTLOOK: Tokenized capital markets could become a core part of financial by the 2030s
Conservative forecasts for tokenized real-world
assets (ex stablecoins)
Source: Deutsche Bank Research.
– Tokenization will modernize financial infrastructure rather than eliminate intermediaries. We expect a gradual
transition onto digital rails, with banks retaining key roles across payments, FX, treasury, financing, collateral

and trading settlement.
Assumption, on the positive side reflects…
– So far, tokenization has achieved its greatest scale in stablecoins, with activity concentrated in crypto trading
and payments gaining ground.
– Short-term: Adoption will be driven by tokenized US Treasuries and money market funds, expanding as yield-
bearing collateral in repo and margin arrangements.
– Long-term:  The opportunity extends beyond assets to the infrastructure supporting issuance, settlement,

custody, collateral management and repo markets.
– We are seeing 3 growth catalysts: (i) infrastructure: major financial market infrastructures (DTCC, NYSE,
Nasdaq) are integrating tokenization into core workflows; (ii) liquidity: Growth of regulated on-chain money
(stablecoins and tokenized deposits) providing a trusted settlement foundation; (iii) and regulation: US 2025
GENIUS ACT continues to encourage global digital asset adoption, although the failure of the CLARITY act to

advance yesterday likely rules out progress in this congressional session until Jan 2027. Looking ahead, focus
will turn back to the SEC and CFTC for crypto rules.
…Risks to adoption
– Accelerated liquidity runs: Tokenization may create a new form of systemic liquidity risk that spreads faster than
traditional finance.
– Money market funds yield-chasing: Declining interest rates or rising competition may push MMF issuers into
risker assts, less-liquid assets to boost yield, undermining their safety.

– Crypto contagion: Tight integration with crypto markets via stablecoin reserves and collateral risks transmitting
digital asset volatility to conventional financial markets.
– Operational risks: Smart-contract vulnerabilities, cyberattacks, network outages could halt trading and prevent
access to the underlying assets.
– Financial Crime: Traditional institutions must scale up compliance and AML controls to manage illicit activities
on decentralized networks.

## Document 290: Meta的灵感Muse，只比“AI点咖啡”高明一点点

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta的灵感Muse，只比“AI点咖啡”高明一点点.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Meta 推出的个⼈ Agent“Muse” 因主打省钱与易⽤登顶榜⾸，其本质仍是通过云端虚拟机与
电脑操作技术执⾏任务，形态与国内早期的 “AI 点咖啡 ” 及 AutoGLM 等产品类似。虽然 Muse
在场景选择和产品体验上更具巧思，但仍⾯临隐私信任、平台封锁（如亚⻢逊拦截）及商
业化变现等⾏业底层难题。
摘要：
Muse 成了巨头 Meta 的 “ 灵感⼥神 ” ，靠⼀⼰之⼒，把巨头 Meta 的股价拉升了 15% 。
Meta 旗下智能体 Muse 9 ⽉ 8 ⽇发布，⾄ 9 ⽉ 18 ⽇，登上美国苹果 App Store 免费榜第⼀，连续三
天占据榜⾸。到 9 ⽉ 21 ⽇，把 Meta 股价推向⾼潮，单⽇上涨 11.4% ，增加约 1923 亿美元市值。
它的热度甚⾄传导到了半导体⾏业，连 CPU 也成了市场追逐的对象，带动 AMD 上涨 9.9% ，市值
⾸次越过 1 万亿美元。
对中国⽤⼾来说，这⼀幕却有些似曾相识。从 “ ⼀句话点外卖 ” 到跨应⽤⽐价、下单，过去⼀年，

个⼈ Agent （能⾃主执⾏任务的 AI 智能体）已经演⽰过不少令⼈兴奋的场景。甚⾄号称天⽣就是
AI 个⼈助⼿的个⼈终端 “ ⾖包⼿机 ” 也出现了，尝试让 AI 直接操作屏幕，替⽤⼾在不同 App 之间完
成任务。
很多⼈都喝过发布会上 “AI 请客 ” 的咖啡。
但现实的体验还是⾚裸裸地残酷。发布会演⽰中流畅的连续操作，到了⽇常使⽤中，可能卡在
登录、⽀付或⼀次突然出现的弹窗上；银⾏等应⽤的限制让 AI 助⼿也完全绕不过去。
如今 Muse 带着相似的产品承诺登场，甚⾄很快遭到 Amazon 封锁。为什么这些熟悉的能⼒和障
碍，到了美国市场，⼜被赋予了如此⼤的想象空间？
图： Meta ⾸席⼈⼯智能官  Alexandr Wang （汪滔）和 Muse ，图⽚由 AI ⽣成
让⼈下载的理由，是 “ 帮我找回钱 ”
Muse 于 9 ⽉ 8 ⽇发布，随后⼗天登顶。传播中反复出现的主⻆，是退款、闲置订阅和保险账单。
Meta ⾸席 AI 官 Alexandr Wang 推动 “MuseMoneyChallenge” （ Muse 省钱挑战），⿎励分享省钱结

果。外媒报道，⼀名创业者称， Muse 帮他找到⼀年便宜 3500 美元的⻋险⽅案；但是，同时，报
道也提醒，这⾥⾯的许多分享其实来⾃ Meta 员⼯。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

但是这类案例给了⽤⼾可以做尝试的理由：检查有没有⽩交的钱。对于不关⼼ AI 的⼈，节省⾦额
⽐⼀串模型的 benchmark 成绩更容易理解，也更适合转发。
Muse 的产品传播还在解决另⼀个障碍：⽤⼾不知道该交给 AI 什么任务。 Muse 设计团队披露，早
期测试中，功能太多反⽽让⼈⽆从下⼿，于是他们加⼊了根据⽬标和对话主动⽣成建议的设
计。科技记者 Alex Heath 也在公开帖⽂中肯定 Ideas 和 Feed ⻚⾯，因为它们会提出⾃⼰想不到的
任务。
沃顿商学院教授 Ethan Mollick 的评价是：其他 AI 实验室的系统已经能完成类似事情， Muse 的专
注设计让普通⼈更容易上⼿；Meta 同时投⼊了⼤量免费算⼒⽀持体验。
C 端的玩法，显然 Meta 更擅⻓，给⽤⼾提供直⽩的吸引⼒ “ 主打省钱 ” ，功能设计上让⽤⼾简单上
⼿，⼊⼝是最熟悉的 whatsapp ，更重要的是还有不花钱的 token 可以挥霍。⽤⼾可以先免费试⽤
Muse ，也能像给联系⼈发消息⼀样，在 WhatsApp ⾥让它处理任务，降低了尝试和使⽤的⻔

槛。
省钱案例、任务建议和熟悉的⼊⼝，构成了这轮传播的⼏个值得关注的环节。⽽且，省钱故事
提供了传播导⽕索，传导到榜单的表现上⼜成为投资者重新评估 Meta 的信号。
技术的新意，究竟落在哪⾥？
那么， Muse 究竟有没有技术上的新意呢？
模型负责理解要求、安排步骤，再调⽤浏览器等⼯具打开⽹⻚、填写表单。这台电脑运⾏在
Meta 的服务器上，因此⽤⼾关掉⼿机应⽤，任务仍可继续。 Meta 通过虚拟机技术，为每位⽤⼾
划出独⽴的运⾏空间，保存个⼈数据和任务进度。 Computer Use （电脑操作能⼒）可以让 AI 能
够使⽤这台电脑上的软件。
Muse 的 “ 云端电脑 ” 已经有先例。根据 OpenAI 官⽅⽂档明确， ChatGPT Work 运⾏在基于虚拟机
的沙盒中，执⾏状态关联⽤⼾，环境可跨任务复⽤，也可在保留部分状态后更换。其云端浏览
器在⽤⼾关闭电脑后仍能继续⼯作。 GPT-6 还可以编写代码操作浏览器与桌⾯，配合插件完成任
务。
但是 Muse 的⼯程特点，是围绕个⼈数据划定权限边界。按 Meta 披露，每位⽤⼾拥有 Muse

Secure VM （ Muse 安全虚拟机），作为个⼈数据的主要存储位置。执⾏程序和⼯具被放进虚拟
机内部的受限容器，账⼾凭据、安全检查及持久状态分别管理；模型推理通过接⼝调⽤外部基
础设施，并⾮整个⼤模型都装在个⼈虚拟机⾥。
我们可以把它理解为⼀个分区的⼯作间， Agent 能在操作区读⽂件、运⾏程序，账⼾凭据由另⼀
处保管，连接器动作和⽹络访问还要经过 Sentinel （权限审核智能体）。这样设计，是为了让处
理陌⽣⽹⻚的程序即使受到误导，也难以直接拿到全部权限。但是，实际安全效果仍需测试验
证。
这样设计的⼯程价值，在于将执⾏能⼒与授权边界⼀同组织起来：服务已有接⼝时调⽤连接
器，没有合适接⼝时尝试浏览器操作。接⼝需要合作接⼊，界⾯操作则要应对⻚⾯变化与验
证。
这套设计服务于持续委托，保留进度，按时间或相关事件继续处理事务，在关键动作前请求授
权。独⽴虚拟机、容器和权限审核都有既有技术基础；⽬前⻚还⽆法证明 Muse 的整套⽅案领先
所有竞争者。

### Page 3

图： Muse 安全架构⽰意图。每位⽤⼾拥有独⽴的云端虚拟机；任务执⾏程序与账⼾凭据、持久
数据分开管理， Sentinel 负责审核对外访问及连接器操作。
国内很早就做过类似形态的产品尝试， 2025 年的 AutoGLM 2.0 同时提供云⼿机、云电脑，覆盖
移动应⽤、⽹⻚与办公任务；⾖包⼿机直接买点就是 “AI 原⽣⼿机 ” ，可以操作⽤⼾⼿机⾥的
app ；千问 app 利⽤阿⾥本⾝的⽣态优势，将点单、⽀付等服务通过合作接⼝接⼊，减少逐屏点
击。
界⾯操作与接⼝调⽤可以结合，任务也能在本机或云端执⾏。 AutoGLM 的 “ 云电脑 ” 指供 AI 操作的
环境， Muse 的 “ 独⽴ Linux 虚拟机 ” 主要描述它怎样为⽤⼾划分运⾏空间，两者处在不同的⽐较层
⾯。
Muse 说明，部分 AI ⼿机体验可以通过现有⼿机和云端服务交付；要调⽤⼿机⾥的应⽤、相机和
系统权限，仍需设备端⽀持与⽤⼾授权。
看起来， Muse 本质还是那个国内⽤⼾熟知的，擅⻓表演点咖啡的 AI 助⼿。只是，这个 AI 助⼿在

国内，暂时没有办法点燃⽤⼾和资本的兴奋点了。
换⼀种思路，不⽌点咖啡
Muse ⾯向个⼈，当前主推⽣活管理，但它也能⽣成⽂档和⽹⻚。 “ 个⼈⽤⼾ ” 和 “ ⽣活任务 ” 并⾮同
⼀个概念，⼀个⼈的助⼿完全可以同时处理家庭与⼯作。
中国也没有放弃⽣活 Agent 。阿⾥披露，千问春节期间促成近 2 亿笔订单；活动伴随 30 亿元激
励，订单量不能单独证明⾃然需求与盈利。与此同时，⾖包在 6 ⽉推出⾯向复杂办公的专业版，
连续包⽉ 68 元起，腾讯推出 WorkBuddy 企业版。国内在并⾏探索⽣活与办公场景，只是办公场
景的⻛⼝更⼤。

### Page 4

办公更容易 “ 收费 ” ，整理⼀批表格、完成调研，节省的劳动可以与省掉的交付时间做⽐较。但是
点⼀杯奶茶原本只需⼏次点击， AI 带来的增量便利有限，出错后接⼿反⽽增加⿇烦。对服务平
台⽽⾔，这类任务仍可能通过订单转化产⽣价值。
Muse在场景的选择上更聪明，⽐如取消忘记的订阅、⽐较续保报价、追踪退款、协调家庭⽇
程。这些事频率未必⾼，但是能省钱、省时间和省⼼，潜⼒在于替⽤⼾处理⻓期拖延的事务，
避免漏掉截⽌时间。
Muse 设计者举过⼀个亲⾝案例：助⼿整理孩⼦开学邮件与学校⽹站时，发现⼀项体育选拔报名
即将截⽌，及时提醒了她。
反观国内的⽣活服务已有不少便捷流程，这可能压低常规点单的增量价值。但保险续费、 App 订
阅、售后申诉、照护协调这些价值，仍然值得去验证。
图： Muse 运⾏的官⽅演⽰视频
这⻔⽣意真的成⽴了吗？
Stratechery 创始⼈ Ben Thompson 在 9 ⽉ 21 ⽇表⽰， Muse 是他试过最容易上⼿的个⼈ Agent 。它
使⽤的 Muse Spark 1.3 尚未达到最前沿模型⽔平，却已能⽀撑很好的产品体验。在他看来，⽤

⼾⼀旦把账⼾信息交给助⼿，并让它参与⽇常事务，更换产品的成本就会提⾼。
Meta 同时也研发基础模型。这轮热度更直接地反映了市场对模型 + 应⽤消费应⽤前景的重新估
值，当模型能⼒达到可⽤⽔平后， Meta 能否凭借产品体验和分发渠道，让⽤⼾持续委托任务，
并从中获得收⼊。 Truist 分析师预测， Muse 到 2030 年可为 Meta 带来 285 亿美元增量收⼊。
Oppenheimer 分析师 Jason Helfstein 维持中性判断：付费⼈群可能已有 ChatGPT 或 Gemini 订
阅，信任也是⻔槛，需要约 1.15 亿付费⽤⼾，才能显著影响 Meta 盈利；模型采⽤每⽉ 20 美元和
80% 增量营业利润率假设。免费算⼒越多，利润假设就越难达到。
但这也从侧⾯解释了⾏情为何能外溢：市场在重估消费 Agent 的规模。 CPU 和内存同样受这种预
期影响。模型推理之外，浏览器、代码和调度需要 CPU（中央处理器）；维持运⾏中的程序需要
DRAM （服务器运⾏内存）。并发任务越多，两类负载可能越⼤。

⽤⼾没有任务时， Meta 可以暂停云端虚拟机，把任务进度保存到硬盘；收到新任务后再恢复。
因此， “ 每⼈⼀台虚拟机 ” 不意味着每⼈全天占⽤固定的 CPU 和内存。 Muse 的热度让投资者预期
更多⼈会使⽤这类服务，但实际需要采购多少芯⽚，还取决于有多少任务同时运⾏、每项任务
要运⾏多久。

### Page 5

Muse 还是没有解决根本难题
Muse 把⽤⼾的数据和执⾏环境放进专属云端虚拟机，但是⽤⼾敢不敢真的让它⻓期接触邮件、

过私⼈数据暴露问题。 Meta 确认，曾为提⾼安全性推迟了原定 4 ⽉的发布。
即使⽤⼾愿意授权， Agent 也未必能进⼊所有服务。 9 ⽉ 21 ⽇， Amazon 阻⽌ Muse 代⽤⼾浏览和
购买商品，理由是这种访问和交易未经许可。⾖包⼿机此前也因登录和⻛控问题，被迫下暂停

可，平台依然有能⼒决定 Agent 能否进⼊⾃⼰的服务。

## Document 297: Oil and gas_ A fragile market equilibrium maintains elevated price levels (2026-09-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Oil and gas_ A fragile market equilibrium maintains elevated price levels (2026-09-03).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-03
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Oil & gas: A fragile market equilibrium maintains elevated price levels
03/09/2026 6
August 2026: Crude oil prices stabilize at a
high level
August 2026: Persisting tensions on the
European gas market
August 2026: Heatwaves fuel wholesale
electricity prices inflation in Europe
Oil and gas markets remain volatile and followed different trajectories during August. The gas market does not benefit
from the buffers in place in the crude oil market. Oil prices have stabilised at a high level, widening the gap compared to

the 2022 crisis. While the increase in gas prices remains lower than that seen following Russia's invasion of Ukraine, the
pace of the price increase is high.
Oil: Brent oil prices stabilised at a high level during August, in the absence of further military escalation and supported by
increased flows passing through the Strait (bypass pipelines and trade shuttle (tanker convoy escorted by US navy)). The
spread between the Brent futures price and the physical price (Dated Brent) has remained narrow since early June, driven

by the use of bypass, the accelerated drawdown on OECD inventories (notably US SPRs), and declining Chinese oil
imports.
Gas: European spot gas prices (TTF) have risen significantly over the past month for two main reasons: 1/ the lack of
recovery in LNG flows exiting the Gulf (unlike crude oil), and 2/ increased competition in the LNG market between Europe,
which is replenishing its pre-winter stocks, and Asia, where demand remains high during the summer months.

Electricity: The rise in European wholesale electricity prices accelerated during the summer due to successive heatwaves.
These heatwaves increased electricity demand and, furthermore, nuclear power production was constrained by
heatwaves and drought conditions in certain Central European countries and in France to a lesser extent. In this context,
the reliance on more expensive resources (gas) is driving electricity prices upward.

## Document 318: Switching 9 to 5s Job switching has picked up, with Gen Z leading gains and gender gaps narrowing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Switching 9 to 5s Job switching has picked up, with Gen Z leading gains and gender gaps narrowing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13016291     1

Economy 
Switching 9 to 5s 
09 September 2026

Key takeaways 
• Job-switching pay premiums have risen to their highest level in more than three years, indicating employers may be willing to 
pay for workers with specific skills, according to Bank of America deposit account data. Gen Z continue to benefit the most from 
changing jobs, while workers paid by the hour are also making particularly large gains from switching. 
• Women's lead in wage gains from job changes has narrowed as men's switching premiums have accelerated faster, returning the 
gap to roughly 2018 levels. Meanwhile, pay disruption rates have fallen and converged across genders, although men's higher 
overall pay levels mean broader earnings gaps remain. 
• AI adoption is not yet translating into broad-based labor displacement, according to BofA Global Research. Job switching 
remains resilient, and industries with the highest AI usage do not yet show consistently weaker labor demand. If that changes, 
younger workers and women look most exposed.

Pay increase associated with a job change hits highest rate since 2023 
The broader US labor market momentum picked up in August (read more on this in the August Institute Employment Report), and 
Bank of America data suggests worker mobility may be showing early signs of improvement. In July, increases in pay associated 
with a job change reached their highest rate in over three years, according to Bank of America account data (Exhibit 1). 
Importantly, the annual average for these pay premiums has inched upwards after consistent yearly declines. 
While job-switching pay premiums have improved in recent months, they remain below pre-pandemic levels. This suggests 
today’s labor market differs from both the Great Resignation period in 2022 and the tighter labor markets of the late 2010s.  
Exhibit 1: In July, the pay increase associated with a job change reached the highest rate in over three years

### Page 2

2 09 September 2026

I NSTI TUTE

Jobs with weekly paychecks see accelerated pay bumps for job switchers 
The premium for switching jobs is most notable among workers with weekly paychecks, though this could also be reflective of 
increased overtime (Exhibit 2). Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) employer payroll data indicates that weekly pay schedules are 
disproportionately concentrated in industries with large hourly workforces and variable hours, particularly construction, leisure 
and hospitality, transportation and warehousing, and portions of retail trade. These jobs often skew lower income. In contrast, 
professional, financial, and other predominantly salaried industries are more likely to use biweekly or semi-monthly payroll 
schedules.  
This acceleration among workers paid weekly provides further evidence that One Big Beautiful Bill Act-related changes to 
overtime hours and tax withholdings could be fueling lower-income wage growth and narrowing differences in spending growth 
across income groups (read more on this in August’s Consumer Checkpoint).  
Exhibit 2: For workers with a weekly paycheck who switched jobs, their change in pay was almost 4x that of those who stayed in their job in July 
After-tax pay change for workers with weekly paychecks by type (monthly, three-month moving average, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Gen Z job change rate outpaces other generations 
Looking across generations, Gen Z have continued to see the greatest pay bumps from switching roles, some of which may 
reflect where they are positioned in the wage distribution (Exhibit 3). Younger workers generally begin their careers at lower 
wage levels, creating more opportunities for substantial percentage increases when they switch jobs. As a result, job changes 
can generate larger proportional pay gains than those typically experienced by older workers. 
Encouragingly, the rate at which Gen Z change jobs has grown and accelerated year-over-year (YoY) since February, surpassing 
all other generations for the first time since 2021 (Exhibit 4). This uptick is particularly noteworthy because it comes at a time 
when many broader labor market indicators suggest slower hiring.  
Gen Z's willingness to keep changing jobs may indicate that opportunities remain available for early-career workers even as 
employers become more selective overall. On the other hand, it could also suggest that companies are prioritizing lower-cost 
entry-level hires over replacing more experienced workers.

### Page 3

I NSTI TUTE

Women's job-switching premium is growing more slowly than men's 
Women are still getting a bigger raise than men when they switch jobs, but the difference between women’s and men’s pay 
change from a job change is back to 2018 levels (Exhibit 5). And though women used to change jobs at a faster rate, men’s job 
change rate has edged out women’s since 2024, according to Bank of America account data. 
In our view, the narrowing difference does not necessarily indicate weaker labor market outcomes for women. Instead, it appears 
to reflect faster recent acceleration in men's job-switching wage gains. As a result, the job-switching premium has become more 
similar across genders than it was during much of the post-pandemic period. 
However, the shrinking gap here could pose challenges for pay equality because women’s level of pay often starts from a lower 
base compared to men’s (read more on this in March’s Where are women positioned in a “K-shaped” economy?). Since job 
switching is often one of the fastest ways workers increase earnings, differences in job-switching premiums can have enduring 
effects on wage growth over time. 
Exhibit 5: The difference between women’s and men’s pay change from a job change is back to 2018 levels 
After-tax pay change from a job change by gender (annual average year-to-date, % difference)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Gender gap in pay disruptions narrows to near record low 
Similarly, the gap between women’s and men’s pay disruption rate – which we view as a proxy for temporary or permanent labor 
force departures – has narrowed and both rates have moderated since the start of the year (Exhibit 6) (see Methodology - Pay 
Disruption Rate). The labor market has become more equal on this measure, but not because women's disruption rates have 
fallen dramatically. Rather, men's disruption rates have increasingly resembled women's. 
Interestingly, this is against the backdrop of fewer men in the labor force. Since January, data from the BLS indicates men’s 
growth in the civilian labor force aged 25-54 has fallen, whereas women’s has continued to increase (Exhibit 7).

I NSTI TUTE

AI anxiety is not yet evident in job change rate 
Job-switching behavior may be an important early indicator because labor market adjustments often show up in mobility before 
employment. If firms become more cautious about hiring in the wake of AI adoption, workers may first become less willing or 
less able to switch jobs before broader effects emerge in payroll or unemployment data.  
So, the fact that we’re seeing an increase in job switching is a sign of a somewhat tight labor market despite overall payrolls 
slowing.  
Information and finance/insurance show early signs of AI labor demand impact 
Using a measure of AI usage from the Census Bureau's Business Trend and Outlook Survey (BTOS), which asks respondents if 
they've used AI in the last two weeks for any business function, BofA Global Research found two industries worth watching: 
information as well as finance and insurance. Both have high AI usage and have seen labor demand decline over the five-month 
period ending in June (Exhibit 8). 
However, the lack of a clear relationship in the overall picture suggests AI adoption is not yet translating into broad-based labor 
displacement. In many industries, businesses may still be experimenting with AI tools or using them to augment workers rather 
than replace them.  
Exhibit 8: At this time, there is not a clear relationship between AI usage levels and growth in labor demand overall

Women and younger workers face the greatest AI-related labor market risks 
Although there is no firm AI impact yet, women are more exposed to AI than men, largely because they are overrepresented in 
administrative roles with routine, language-based tasks well suited to generative AI automation or augmentation, according to 
the International Labor Organization (read more on this in May’s Adaptability is the new job security: AI and the future of work). 
BLS forecasts confirm this – demand is expected to be limited for occupations such as procurement clerks; legal secretaries and 
administrative assistants; and customer service representatives. 
And specifically in AI-related roles, women made up just over a quarter of new hires in the US last year — compared with half of 
new hires in non-AI occupations — according to data from a LinkedIn study.1 This suggests that women could be more 
susceptible to the possible negative impact of AI on jobs in more ways than one. 
Younger workers also look potentially exposed because they are more likely to hold roles with automatable tasks and where AI 
adoption is occurring more rapidly (read more on Gen Z’s labor market position in August’s Gen Z reality check). Still, certain 
roles with very high AI exposure2 for recent graduates are expected to have strong growth over the next ten years, according to 
BLS employment occupation projections (Exhibit 9).

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Bank of America credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card holders 
making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
If applicable, the consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level.  
If applicable, any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a 
deposit account or credit card. Any reference to aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash and checks.  
Median annual income growth is derived from customers who have a valid income value for every month over the time period and who have a 
non-null gender code. Gender data is self-select. 
The Pay Disruption Rate is defined as the proportion of customers who previously had 12 months of regular payroll payments into their 
accounts, followed by at least 3 and up to 6 months of no payments, relative to the total number of customers with 12 consecutive months of 
payroll. 
The job-to-job change rate (j2j rate) is defined as the proportion of customers with an identified change in their employer as a proportion of 
the total number of customers with employment income. We estimate the median pay rise associated with a j2j change using the pay in the 
first three months of the new job compared to the same three months a year ago who have a non-null gender code.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Economist, Bank of America Institute

## Document 339: U.S. Economic Outlook_ September 2026 - Economic Resilience Raises the Rate Floor

Source: U.S. Department of Commerce, U.S. Department of Labor, IHS Markit, Federal Reserve Board and Wells Fargo Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• Consumer spending is holding up, and we expect real PCE to rise at around a 3.0% annualized rate
in Q3.
Consumer spending has been mixed, yet resilient in recent months. After a soft July, driven largely by

weaker durable goods purchases, retail sales rebounded strongly in August, rising at a 1.2% monthly
clip amid broadly distributed gains across retailers. The latest data have led us to lift our near-term
growth forecast, and we now look for real PCE to rise at a 3.2% annualized clip in Q3. Previous data
will also likely be revised higher on firmer control group sales data and strong services revenue in Q2,
suggesting recent consumer momentum remains intact. All told, the consumer is still in decent shape

and has capacity to keep spending. Real income growth remains modest but is beginning to improve
as inflation eases and the labor market remains broadly supportive suggesting continued consumption
even if that pace of spending may moderate into year-end.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build-out is still king in terms of the contribution to business investment, but the
broadening in capex spending beyond the tech sector is taking root.

AI-related investment continues to dominate capital allocation. There are no signs of firms scaling
back their outlays now, though at some point as AI investment matures, merely sustaining spending
at elevated levels will provide a smaller incremental boost to growth than it has in recent quarters.
Beyond AI, the tentative signs we've identified that signal a broadening in the investment backdrop
are getting a bit louder. The ISM manufacturing index has been in expansion territory for eight-

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
straight months, and durable goods orders have firmed across a wider range of industries. Part of
this improvement likely reflects delayed capital spending plans finally moving forward, but inventory
rebuilding after five consecutive quarters of drawdown may be lending some support as well.

Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Investment: Residential
• Residential construction looks likely to taper through the rest of the year while mortgage rates and
new home inventories remain elevated.
Headwinds have intensified against residential construction. The trend in single-family construction is
downshifting, emblematic of the affordability challenges depressing buyer demand. As of this writing,

mortgage rates have risen to over 7.0% and builder confidence has declined alongside. Sales stuck in
a low gear mean that new home listings are inching higher, further discouraging new development.
Multifamily construction appears to be holding up but faces limited potential upside with vacancy
rates elevated across much of the South. We still see a potential for marginal improvement in 2027,
but higher mortgage rates dampen our outlook.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures

• Construction appears to be gaining steam, led by data centers and power. However, sluggish
commercial starts elsewhere and high interest rates are likely to keep growth relatively modest.
The AI infrastructure build out is supporting modest growth in nonresidential construction. After
moderating for a full year, private nonresidential outlays rose from April to July. Data centers and
power are propelling current momentum. Though construction has also strengthened across sports

facilities and communication infrastructure, commercial projects remain stuck in low gear. Categories
like warehousing and retail remain soft, and overall architectural billings have improved only modestly.
Looking ahead, a recent breakout in megaprojects is likely to support additional data center and
manufacturing development, while higher interest rates limit overall growth.

Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports are tracking to slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth amid
strong imports.
Imports are shaping up for another strong quarter after a surge in July and a rebound in August
retail spending suggesting continued domestic demand for product. The July import gain was again

almost entirely driven by high-tech related goods like computers & accessories, semiconductors and
telecommunications equipment. We still anticipate a bit more of a broadening out in imports to come,
perhaps as early as August as businesses replenish inventory amid continued consumer demand.
Export growth is a bit more constrained, suggesting an expansion in the trade deficit and that net
exports will slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth in Q3.

Labor Market
• We modestly raised our 2026 payroll forecast and made no material changes to our 2027 forecast.
We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end this year at 4.2%.
August's employment report reinforced that the labor market remains stable. Payroll growth has
averaged 106K over the past six months, though we expect some moderation in the months ahead.
Hiring measures from regional Fed surveys and hiring plans from small businesses have cooled from

their early summer jumps and job openings have leveled off. At the same time, initial jobless claims
remain near multi-decade lows and continue to indicate that layoffs remain contained. We expect
payroll growth to moderate to roughly 65K per month over the remainder of the year, a pace broadly
consistent with our estimate of breakeven. As hiring cools slightly and the participation rate rebounds
somewhat further, we see some scope for the unemployment rate to edge up to 4.2% by year-end.

Even with the slight increase though, labor market conditions would remain consistent with full
employment.
Inflation
• Incorporating our expectations for the upcoming BEA methodology changes and recent inflation
data, we look for the core PCE deflator to end the year at 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis, up from
3.1% in our previous forecast.
Headline inflation remains tied to the conflict in the Middle East, although underlying price pressures
continue to move in a more favorable direction. Higher oil prices are keeping some upward pressure

on energy-sensitive categories in the near term, but tariff-related goods inflation is fading and price
pressures outside a handful of technology-related categories remain limited. Meanwhile, core services
inflation remains on a moderating trend as shelter inflation continues to gradually cool and labor cost
pressures slowly ease. Taken together, inflation pressures appear less widespread than earlier this year,
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
but recurring supply shocks are likely to keep the path back to 2% bumpy. We expect the core PCE
deflator to slow to 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis this year.

Fiscal Policy
• We forecast this year's federal budget deficit will total $2.1 trillion (~6.5% of GDP), and next year's
will be about the same in dollar terms and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).

The federal budget deficit continues to hover in the 6.0-6.5% of GDP range, similar to where it was
at the end of the Biden administration. The outlook for the next couple of years is one of status quo.
We doubt there will be any new, big fiscal legislation with divided government likely after the midterm
elections, but rising interest costs and steady growth in mandatory spending programs such as Social
Security and Medicare will keep deficits from shrinking much. It's not just U.S. fiscal problems putting

upward pressure on long-term rates. Other factors such as robust corporate debt issuance, policy
uncertainty and foreign factors are also playing a role. Our base case for 6.0%-6.5% of GDP budget
deficits will not help when it comes to bringing down long-term interest rates.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

U.S. Economic Outlook: September 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• We have changed our fed funds forecast to 4.00-4.25% by year-end 2026, with no changes in
2027. Our year-end forecasts for the 10-year Treasury yield are also higher at 4.90% and 4.75%,
respectively, for this year and next.
The FOMC made clear at its September 16 meeting that stubbornly high PCE inflation this year was
the key factor behind the Committee's decision to raise rates. Given this, we think only a string of

good inflation data can bail the Fed out, and accordingly we think one more rate hike is coming this
year, with softer inflation data in 2027 giving way to a more patient FOMC. We could easily envision
one more hike beyond that or a "one and done" hike where the FOMC does not go again. Outcomes
outside that range, however, strike us as unlikely. A rising neutral rate makes meaningful rate cuts hard
to justify in the absence of an economic slowdown, while the case for a more aggressive tightening

cycle seems weak given that the labor market is not overheating and much of the excess inflation is
supply side driven. We think a very short gradual tightening cycle and an eventual reduction in energy
prices/slowdown in inflation will allow Treasury yields to drift modestly lower in 2027. But, the factors
mentioned above (e.g., large budget deficits, rising neutral rate) should prevent yields from falling in a
bigger way.

International Developments
• Global growth has proved more resilient than expected, prompting us to upgrade our 2026 global
GDP growth forecast to 2.9%. We have also modestly revised our global inflation outlook higher,
lifting our 2026 CPI forecast to 4.4% and our 2027 forecast accordingly.
• We now expect more tightening across advanced economies than in our previous monthly forecast
update, while emerging market policy paths remain mixed.

Global economic activity proved more resilient than expected in H1-2026, with early indicators
suggesting that momentum carried into Q3. While precautionary stockpiling supported some of the
earlier strength, activity has remained resilient on firmer services, continued AI-related investment and
exports (U.S., South Korea, Japan and Australia) and increased defense spending (Eurozone and Japan).
We expect inflation to remain higher for longer before easing next year and averaging 4% in 2027 as

tighter financial conditions weigh on demand and supply pressures fade. Risks remain skewed to the
upside. Oil and product inventories remain below pre-conflict levels, while limited refining capacity and
ongoing disruptions leave energy prices vulnerable to renewed pressure. El Niño and higher fertilizer
costs threaten food prices, while AI-related demand is raising semiconductor costs. Second-round
effects remain limited, but successive supply shocks increase the risk of persistent inflation into next

year.

## Document 345: US inflation, payrolls data support rate hike this week

Source PDF: all report pdf/US inflation, payrolls data support rate hike this week.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

PUBLIC 
US inflation, payrolls data 
support rate hike this week 
Summary 
Event: US core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, beating 
consensus expectations for a 0.2% rise. The inflation report 
follows a stronger-than-expected jobs report for August. The 
combination supports a Fed rate hike this week. However, 
annual core inflation eased to 2.4% y/y, from 2.5% , 
sustaining the disinflationary trend. Therefore, a Fed rate hike 
this week is likely to be one-off, not the start of a hiking cycle.  
Market reaction:  Bond and FX markets were relatively 
muted, while the S&P500 index jumped 0.9%. The policy rate 
sensitive US 2 -year yield rose 4bps to 4.63% as markets 
factored in almost 90% chance of rate hike this week. US 10-
year and 30-year yields were  little changed  at 4.97% and 
5.35%, respectively . The USD index (DXY) rose 0. 1%, 
staying close to its 200-day moving average. 
Investment strategy: The latest inflation report for August 
reinforces the case for a rate hike at this week's Fed policy 
meeting. As signalled in our previous CIO publications , 
“Rising Risk of a Fed Rate Hike” and “Inching towards a Fed 
‘credibility’ hike”, we now believe there is 90% chance that 
the Fed will raise the Fed Funds Rate in September.  
We view this more as a “credibility” hike by the Fed due to 
Chair Warsh’s hawkish rhetoric at the Fed’s recent annual 
Jackson Hole summit. US c ore consumer inflation , while 
hotter than expected, continued to move in the right direction 
on an annualised basis, easing to 2.4% y/y, from 2.5%. Also, 
US wage growth continues to slow, as reflected in the latest 
average hourly earnings. As such we do not believe this is 
the beginning of a cycle of multiple rate hikes by the Fed.  
Risks to the view:  Headline inflation accelerated to 0.4% 
m/m and stayed elevated at +3.4% y/y. Energy prices, driven 
by the Middle East conflict, were a primary contributor, with 
oil prices having surged in the days preceding the print. The 
producer inflation report released last week also showed 
producer prices rising 0.4% m/m, the most since May, and 
5.4% y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
 US stocks rose, while bond yields and the USD were 
little changed, after a slightly hotter-than-expected 
inflation report cemented the chance of a Fed rate hike 
Asset performance: 10-Sep close to 11-Sep close

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

US core inflation continued to ease in August, despite a 
m/m acceleration. This argues for one rate hike, not the 
start of a multiple rate hiking cycle

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 14 September 2026

PUBLIC 
Moreover, the unexpected rise in the so- called supercore 
(core services less housing) inflation, to 0.5% m/m and 3.0% 
y/y would support the hawks in the Fed in their view that there 
is increasing risk higher energy prices are spilling over to 
service sector prices. We need to watch closely the latest 
acceleration in core services inflation as there is a risk that 
price pressures may be broadening beyond the energy 
sector. If oil prices stay above USD 100/bbl for a prolonged 
period, the Fed may be forced to hike multiple times to 
prevent a de-anchoring of inflation expectations.  
Fed policy implications 
The Fed enters its 15-16 September policy meeting with the 
committee visibly divided (the Fed voted 9- 3 in July to hold 
rates), though the latest US non-farm payrolls and CPI prints 
have tilted the balance toward a hike.  
Several key dynamics are in play:  i) Chairman Warsh faces 
intensifying pressure to raise rates following the print, with the 
report bolstering the argument that underlying inflation 
remains sticky; ii) Governor Waller had explicitly stated his 
September decision would be "heavily influenced" by the 
August inflation data and had signalled openness to a hike if 
inflation did not show continued progress toward the 2% goal. 
He also cautioned he did not want to "raise rates into 
disinflation"; iii) Governor Barr said the Fed should raise rates 
if inflation is not moderating, citing PCE inflation holding at 
3.7% y/y in July, well above the 2% target; and iv) New York 
Fed President Williams had taken a more measured tone 
prior to the print, noting inflation was continuing to trend down 
and that tariff effects were moving into the rearview mirror.  
A notable political dimension: President Trump publicly 
demanded the Fed lower rates following the August jobs 
report, intensifying the political pressure on the institution.  
Bottom Line 
The August inflation print, particularly the above- consensus 
core reading, which follows the much stronger-than-expected 
payrolls data, effectively seals expectations for a 25bps rate 
hike at the 15- 16 September Fed meeting. The data did not 
provide the dovish cover needed for the more cautious 
members of the committee to hold, and Chairman Warsh 
faces a politically charged but data-supported case for action.  
The more consequential question for markets is now the Fed 
press conference: whether Warsh signals this as a one-and-
done move or maintains a tightening bias given inflation still 
running above the 2% target. Should the Fed hike this week  
and sound hawkish, it would likely be USD bullish and gold 
bearish in the near term.  
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 346: US manufacturing revival gathers pace despite tariff and energy headwinds

Source PDF: all report pdf/US manufacturing revival gathers pace despite tariff and energy headwinds.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 24 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  24 September 2026   UNITED STATES
US manufacturing revival gathers pace
despite tariff and energy headwinds
After three years of stagnation, US manufacturing appears to be on an
upswing as production and order books gain momentum. Tariffs and
energy costs pose challenges, but trends in reshoring, AI/robotic
technologies and rising defence spending provide reasons for longer-

term optimism
The revival in US manufacturing is being led by the tech sector, including Google data centres (pictured
above)
The US manufacturing sector has experienced a torrid couple of decades. Output volumes
remain 10% below the peak level hit just before the Global Financial Crisis in December 2007.
The sector employs a million fewer workers than it did 20 years ago. Reasons for this poor
performance include consumer demand preferences shifting ever further toward services over

physical goods, underinvestment in advanced production facilities relative to international
competitors, high US labour costs and the removal of trade barriers allowing foreign
manufacturers to gain market share.
Manufacturing output levels and manufacturing employment

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 2
Source: Macrobond, ING
Nonetheless, the sector has shown genuine signs of a revival over the past year. The ISM
production index has moved from sub-50 contraction territory to signalling robust growth
ahead. Below, we look at what is driving this turnaround and how it could evolve.
ISM production index points to an ongoing manufacturing
output upturn
Source: Macrobond, ING
Tariffs remain President Trump’s primary tool

Reviving manufacturing has been top of mind for all recent presidents. In his first term,
President Trump’s solution was to impose tariffs on Chinese-made imports along with sector-
specific levies. The rationale being that this would boost the price competitiveness of US
manufacturers in key sectors. Yet it failed to generate the desired turnaround. Initiatives from
President Biden similarly yielded little benefit.
In his second term, President Trump doubled down, initially announcing 25% tariffs on steel

and aluminium, and then a 25% tariff on auto parts before launching into his 'Liberation Day'
tariffs in April 2025 that dramatically expanded coverage.

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 3
Invoked under the International Emergency Economic Powers Act (IEEPA), individual tariff rates
changed based on a trading partner's willingness to open markets to US manufacturers and
guarantee investment within the US. The chart below shows that the realised tariff rate
undershot the calculated implied average tariff rate based on the full-year 2024 import mix.
Reasons for this include import substitution via switching to lower-tariffed foreign suppliers, a

surge in tariff-exempt high-tech imports, and better USMCA compliance. This means a greater
proportion of Canadian and Mexican trade qualified for tariff exemptions, and some modest
evidence of switching towards US-made products.
Effective announced tariff rates versus realised tariff rates (%)
Source: Yale budget Lab, Macrobond, ING
The Supreme Court ruled against those tariffs in late February. In response, Trump swiftly
invoked Section 122 of the 1974 Trade Act. It allows tariffs of up to 15% for as long as 150 days

before the US Trade Representative launches Section 301 investigations. The end result is 10%
tariffs on 14 countries, including the EU, Mexico, Canada and the UK, with 46 other countries,
including imports from China, Japan and India facing a 12.5% tariff. This, in itself, is a lower
tariff regime under the IEEPA system.
Given several important tech and medical categories are exempted, we expect the realised
tariff rate to come close to 7%. This is a less onerous environment than 12 months ago.

Moreover, importing companies' cash flows are being boosted by the Supreme Court’s decision
to insist that the $166bn of IEEPA tariffs be fully refunded. That means the realised tariff rate
temporarily fell into negative territory in July, as IEEPA refunds more than offset all other tariff
charges.
Reshoring yet to show up in construction activity
Citing the effectiveness of tariffs, the Administration has touted the “largest reshoring wave in
American history”. It points to a four-year high for the ISM manufacturing index while

### Page 4

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 4
employment in the manufacturing sector rose 58,000 in the first eight months of this year.
Amid pledges from corporate America to invest more in the US, the National Association of
Manufacturers reported that foreign direct investment in US manufacturing reached a new
record level in 2025 of $2.5tr.
Yet manufacturing-related construction activity does not reflect this positive narrative.

Manufacturing construction spending peaked in September 2024 at an annualised $249bn and
currently stands at $170bn. We’ve seen a sharp slowdown in construction activity in the
computer, electronics and electrical sector from $126bn to $57bn over that same period. That
follows the conclusion of the CHIPS and Science Act from the previous Biden Administration,
which provided nearly $53bn to boost the domestic semiconductor industry. Strip out this

sector and construction activity tied to the rest of manufacturing has changed little over the
past two years. So, despite commitments from business leaders, little evidence suggests that a
reshoring boom is underway just yet.
Manufacturing sector construction spending ($bn)
Source: Macrobond, ING
While tariffs may improve the relative cost comparison versus foreign products, the National
Association of Manufacturers states that in 2024, manufacturing employees earned an

average of $106,691, including pay and benefits. That is substantially higher than the average
cost of employing someone in China, South Korea and even Germany. This suggests that for
manufacturing reshoring to make sense, it would be tied to companies that are highly
automated with high value-added production. Alternatively, there could be an appetite to
reshore where consumers are willing to pay the premium for a 'Made in America' label or
where security of supply chains is an imperative.

Sectors most likely to receive a boost include technology, transport and aerospace, electric
vehicles, clean energy and pharmaceuticals. The latter is where we have seen the largest
announcements, with Johnson & Johnson’s $55bn investment spending plan over the next
four years and Eli Lilly announcing a $27bn investment in its US manufacturing operations.

### Page 5

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 5
And in solar manufacturing, tariffs, price floors, and China-focussed trade restrictions are
supporting the expansion of US manufacturing across the value chain.
While the construction numbers aren’t showing any uplift yet, it takes significant time to get
planning in place. Advanced manufacturing plants also take a long time to construct. Intel, for
example, suggests it takes three to five years with 6,000 construction workers at a cost of

$10bn to build a new factory. An aluminium smelter takes two to three years for construction,
in addition to the time needed to design and secure planning approval. Significant
infrastructure is also typically required for all new manufacturing sites, including power, road,
rail and water, adding to the cost of reshoring.
The Middle East conflict and energy prices
Restrictions on shipping through the Strait of Hormuz have raised concerns about energy

shortages, higher prices and potential production disruption for manufacturers globally.
However, the US is better positioned to manage those challenges than European and Asian
competitors. The US produces more energy than it consumes. Oil prices are set in global
markets and push up the cost of gasoline and diesel, with freight costs consequently
increasing in the US and elsewhere. However, the US is in a better position relative to foreign
competitors in terms of natural gas. Abundant domestic production is keeping prices well

below long-run averages.
This has a knock-on effect: lower electricity prices vis-à-vis other countries, which should give
US manufacturers a cost advantage over foreign peers. US-based companies involved with
steel, metal and glass production – heavy industries that require high-temperature processes –
and agriculture and fertiliser production, which use natural gas to convert into nitrogen
fertilisers, should be relative winners on the global stage. Similarly, the availability of natural

gas, used as a feedstock to create products such as plastics, should give a competitive boost
for US manufacturers.
Nevertheless, electricity-intensive manufacturers could come under pressure as AI-driven data
centre growth drives up power demand. For example, Emirates Global Aluminium and Century
Aluminium are planning a 750,000-ton aluminium smelter in Oklahoma, the first new US
smelter in almost 50 years. The facility would double current US aluminium output, but may

face challenges competing with data centres for power.
Financial conditions are a headwind
Borrowing costs have risen in response to elevated energy prices and a belief that the Federal
Reserve is set to tighten policy and keep interest rates higher for longer. In an environment of
substantial government fiscal deficits and anxiety about debt sustainability, US longer-dated
Treasury yields have pushed above 5%. Corporate bond spreads have tightened, but not by

enough to prevent an overall rise in borrowing costs. Moody’s 10Y Aaa corporate debt

### Page 6

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 6
benchmark index is pointing to a 56bp increase in borrowing costs since the start of the year.
Mortgage rates, auto loans and credit card borrowing costs all increased for similar reasons.
This will act as a brake on economic activity over time. Auto loan and credit card delinquencies
are already on par with the rates recorded in the aftermath of the Global Financial Crisis, and
the household saving ratio is around 3pp below its long-run average of 6%. As such, this

tightening of financial conditions, amplified by the recent strengthening of the US dollar, is a
headwind for the sector.
Sector performance diverges markedly
Tariffs and energy security do boost the attractiveness of manufacturing in the US. But so does
US economic growth continuing to outperform other key markets. Between 2023 and 2026,
the US economy has grown an average of 2.5% year-on-year in volume terms versus 0.9% in
Europe.
At the same time, the US Administration believes maintaining the lead in the AI/technology

frontier is imperative for both economic and national security, given competition from China.
Rapid AI adoption is leading to robust construction of data centres, boosting electricity
demand and spurring investment across the power supply chain, from generation and
transmission to electrical equipment. In response, gas turbine manufacturers such as GE
Vernova and Caterpillar are planning major capacity expansions in the US. ABB has also
announced a $110m investment to expand production of switchgear and other electrical

components in Virginia, Puerto Rico, and North Carolina. The Trump administration's focus on
expanding US energy dominance and industrial competitiveness is increasing the appeal of
manufacturing these in the US.
As we can see in the chart below, high value-added or advanced manufacturing, within which
we group tech, electronics, transport and power-related sectors, has significantly
outperformed other sectors. We expect this story to continue. We also see defence as a sector

that should continue to post strong growth. As more countries increase military spending, US
companies may benefit from strengthening order books.
Traditional, labour-intensive, commoditised manufacturing sectors continue to shrink under
pressure from foreign competitors. Here, tariffs are not enough to provide the competitiveness
boost needed to offset the high cost of producing in the US. These US producers are also often
detrimentally impacted in foreign markets by retaliatory tariffs.

Advanced manufacturing versus traditional output (Dec 2007 =
100)

### Page 7

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 7
Source: Macrobond, ING
Modest growth disguises growing bifurcation
Business surveys point to strong order books, and we see this supporting manufacturing’s
ongoing revival. Our volume growth forecast for US manufacturing of 1.5-2% per year over the
next three years may not seem like much, but it should be seen in the context of the sluggish
activity experienced over the past 20 years. This headline forecast also masks increased

bifurcation, with advanced manufacturing growing rapidly while more traditional sectors look
set to retrench further.
Highly automated, high-value-added sectors at the forefront of the AI/technology revolution
are seen as national champions by the government and are able to absorb the relatively high
wages on offer in the US. The desire to incorporate AI advances should, in theory,
drive advances in productivity-enhancing initiatives that further support innovation. As such,

pharma, tech, transport & aerospace, electrical and power-related sectors should continue to
grow robustly.
Lower value-added production, where labour costs make up a greater share of the overall cost
base of production, will continue to struggle unless it can garner a 'made in America' premium.
Heavy industry, such as steel, is likely to be somewhere in the middle. While costs have risen,
they have risen more elsewhere. And the perception of the US as a source of reliable energy,

backed by strong political support in a higher-tariff environment, also presents opportunities
for growth.

《油價與運價維持高檔，航空產業需求遭遇逆風》
近期受到中東衝突升溫導致僅有少量油輪通過荷姆
茲海峽，貨櫃船實際上仍無法通行。過去5-6年全球
港口吞吐運量成長遠低於船舶訂單量成長 ，導致重
大港口的貨櫃塞港嚴重 。中東地緣政治風險和全球
性的港口擁擠預估短期內都未能改善契機 ，因此未
來半年貨櫃運價仍將維持高位 。由於過去半年美國
機票價格已大幅上漲 ，導致價格敏感的美國旅客消
費開始偏向保守。美國運輸安全管理局(簡稱TSA)統
計每日旅客吞吐量近期呈現持續下滑 ，顯示航空業
持續性漲價已經衝擊到旅客需求 。隨著機構上調

4Q26原油預測 10%到88美元，高價位的航空燃油
價格也將再次對全球航空公司運量與獲利造成衝擊 。
《眾議院通過限制擴大對伊朗軍事行動 ，對國防類
股形成壓力》
今年上半年因中東衝突升溫及美伊緊張情勢 ，國防
股曾出現一波強勁上漲 。但隨著美國眾議院 9/15以
220票對204票通過限制總統對伊朗軍事行動的決議
案，要求未獲國會授權前不得持續或擴大對伊朗的
軍事行動 。雖然美國國會決議案主要是限制新增的
軍事行動授權 ，並非削減國防預算 。但美國國會限
制進一步軍事行動 ，已經造成市場資金對國防類股
進行獲利了結 。考量航空類股仍將面臨高油價逆風 ，
以及美國眾議院擴大限制政府對伊朗軍事行動 。本
行對工業產業展望維持中立評等。

美國與歐洲市場 
 美國經濟活動持續擴張，且投入成本與就業同步走強，使市場擔憂通膨壓力難以消退，進一步
加深市場對聯準會可能續升息的疑慮，高評價成長股承受賣壓，風險偏好降溫。 道瓊指數下跌
0.68%、標普 500 指數下跌 0.75%、那斯達克指數下跌 1.13%、費半下跌 1.23%。 
 由於伊朗對荷姆茲海峽航運釋出強硬訊號，再度引發市場對中東衝突升溫及原油供應受阻的擔
憂，帶動國際油價走揚，泛歐 600 指數開高走低，終場下跌 0.44%。 
亞洲與新興市場 
 日本因適逢敬老節假期，自 9 月 21 日至 9 月 23 日休市三日。 
 周三台積電及鴻海走揚，加上台泥受惠成功拓展東歐市場，以及功率元件族群受惠漲價題材帶
動，股價表現亮眼，推升台股早盤一度大漲逾500 點，最高來到48,341.56 點，隨後漲幅收斂，
終場仍上漲 0.75%，收 48,157.29 點，成功站上 48,000 點整數關卡。 
 中國股市週三盤面資金聚焦 PCB、玻璃基板及科學儀器等科技題材；煤炭及傳媒類股則表現疲
弱。兩市成交額縮減至 1.78 兆元人民幣 ， 顯示投資人觀望情緒升溫 ， 終場上證指數下跌0.39%。 
商品市場 
 黃金：受美國 PMI 強於預期、聯準會官員偏鷹發言以及美元走強，提高持有無息資產黃金的
機會成本，進而壓抑金價表現，黃金下跌 1.33%。 
 原油：伊朗對荷姆茲海峽航運釋出強硬訊號，提升能源運輸中斷風險；此外，美國政府研議限
制柴油出口或推動業者自願減少出口，進一步凸顯全球成品油供應偏緊，終場布蘭特原油上漲
3.86%；然而，因美國原油庫存上周意外增加，西德州原油下跌 2.57%。 
債市與外匯 
 美債：美國 9 月綜合 PMI 升至 58.4、創 2021 年以來新高，加上聯準會官員偏鷹發言，以及 5
年期公債標售需求疲弱，強化市場對後續升息的預期，美債殖利率全面走升。 
 美元：受美國 PMI 強於預期與美債殖利率全面上行帶動，市場重新提高對後續升息路徑的押
注，美元指數上漲 0.49%，連續四個交易日上漲。 
資料來源：元大投顧、鉅亨網、Bloomberg

## Document 424: マーケット見通し『向こう１年間の市場見通し』(2026年10月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/マーケット見通し『向こう１年間の市場見通し』(2026年10月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

向こう1年間の市場見通し （9月7日時点）

予 想 コ メ ント
コメント
株式
日本株はイラン情勢悪化に伴う原油高を受けて急落した後、 Ａ Ｉ 関連株にけん引され、 上値を追った。 日米ともに Ａ Ｉ
関連株の業績見通しは著し く上向いており、 この点において株価は業績の裏付けを伴っている。 半導体関連銘柄
は ７ 月に下落した一方、 それ以外の銘柄は堅調に推移している。
ドル円
Ｆ Ｒ Ｂ は2025年後半に累積0.75％ptの利下げを実施した後、 ８ 月までは据え置いている。 利上げが意識されるな
か、 ドル高主導の円安圧力が生じている。 もっ とも、 ７ 月末頃に日米協調介入が実施されたことで、 一方的な円安進
行の可能性は低下した。 当面は連続介入に対する警戒感が残存しよう。
金利
日銀は ６ 月に政策金利を1.0％へと引き上げ、 先行きの利上げ方針も確認した。 インフレ圧力が高まるなか、 円安
に対する警戒もあり、 半年に一度よりも早い頻度で利上げが進む可能性が高まっている。 長期金利はインフレ警
戒感が燻る下、 ２ ％台後半～ ３ ％台前半で推移するとみている。
（注） 記載された内容は、 第一生命ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限りません。
向こう １ 年間の日経平均株価

Overview 
金利上昇が問う財政への信認 
  末吉 孝行 
世界的な金利上昇が続いている。日米独の 10 年国債利回りは、 2025 年末よりそれぞれ
0.94％pt、0.86％pt、0.65％pt 程度上昇した（2026 年 9 月 16 日時点、Bloomberg 調べ）。3 カ
国に共通する背景には、根強いインフレのほか、歳出拡大や政府債務増加への懸念がある。 
日本の中長期的なインフレ率が2％程度で、実質GDP成長率が0％台後半であるとすれば、現
状の 3％弱の長期金利水準に大きな違和感はない。だが問題は水準ではなく上昇のスピードだ。
金利上昇は国債利払費の増加を通じて財政を圧迫する。2027 年度予算に向けた概算要求が総額
約143.1兆円と過去最大になった背景には、利払費が2026年度当初予算から約3.5兆円の増加
となったこともある。 
長期金利が 5％に迫る米国では、2026 会計年度の利払費は前年度から 1,110 億ドル程度増加
するとみられ（米議会予算局調べ） 、連邦政府債務は40 兆ドルを超えた。長期金利の更なる上
昇や利払費の増加を抑制するには、インフレを抑えるだけでなく財政への信認を維持すること
も重要である。イラン戦争の長期化や中間選挙対策によって歳出拡大に向けた圧力が強まる中、
ベッセント財務長官は財政再建パッケージを検討しているが、まだ煮詰まっていないようだ。 
新興国にも悪影響がある。IMF も警告しているように、先進国の金利上昇で新興国債務の利
払費も増加しかねない。新興国には、財政改革に着実に取り組み、投資家からの信認を高める
ことで、先進国の国債利回りに対する自国債務のスプレッドの圧縮に努めてきた国もある。し
かし、先進国の金利上昇で新興国債務の利回りも押し上げられれば、その効果は弱まる。 
先進国政府が政府債務増加と金利上昇に上手に付き合い、財政への信認を維持できるかは、
新興国も含めた今後の世界経済の安定を左右する重要な鍵となろう。
